1:# Workspace Reimplementation Direction
2:
3:Date: 2026-04-30
4:
5:Fresh-agent entry point: read `WORKSPACE_REIMPLEMENTATION_START_HERE.md` first, then return to this document for the full product direction.
6:
7:This document captures the intended direction for reimplementing OpenSpec workspace support from scratch, based on what we learned from the workspace POC.
8:
9:The reimplementation should be ordered around the path a real user takes through OpenSpec:
10:
11:```text
12:set up workspace
13:  -> link repos or folders
14:  -> open workspace
15:  -> explore across repos or folders
16:  -> create proposal
17:  -> apply one repo slice
18:  -> verify
19:  -> archive
20:```
21:
22:The goal is not to rebuild every POC mechanism. The goal is to get one user-facing capability working at a time, in the same order a user would naturally create, implement, verify, and archive a change.
23:
24:## North Star
25:
26:A user should think:
27:
28:```text
29:I have a multi-repo product goal.
30:I set up an OpenSpec workspace.
31:I open it with my agent.
32:The agent can see the linked repos or folders.
33:We explore until the scope is clear.
34:Then we create a proposal.
35:Then we implement one repo slice at a time.
36:```
37:
38:They should not think:
39:
40:```text
41:I need to create a change so repos become visible.
42:I need to materialize repo-local artifacts.
43:I need to understand implementation-specific workspace machinery.
44:I need to manage target metadata separately from proposal files.
45:```
46:
47:The core product rule is:
48:
49:```text
50:Workspace visibility is not change commitment.
51:```
52:
53:Linked repos or folders are planning context. Creating a change is a planning commitment. Applying a change is an implementation workflow.
54:
55:## Build Order
56:
57:### 1. Workspace Setup And Links
58:
59:First make workspace setup boring and solid.
60:
61:User goal:
62:
63:```text
64:Create a planning home and link the repos or folders OpenSpec should know about.
65:```
66:
67:Expected surface:
68:
69:```bash
70:openspec workspace setup
71:openspec workspace setup --no-interactive --name platform --link /path/to/api --link web=/path/to/web
72:openspec workspace list
73:openspec workspace ls
74:openspec workspace link /path/to/api
75:openspec workspace link api-service /path/to/api
76:openspec workspace relink api /new/path/to/api
77:openspec workspace doctor
78:```
79:
80:Expected outcome:
81:
82:```text
83:workspace-folder/
84:  changes/
85:  .openspec-workspace/
86:    workspace.yaml
87:    local.yaml
88:```
89:
90:Product decisions:
91:
92:- Use `.openspec-workspace/`, not `.openspec/`, for workspace metadata.
93:- Keep `changes/` visible in the workspace folder.
94:- Keep setup as the only public creation path for the first release; do not expose `workspace create`.
95:- Use `workspace link` and `workspace relink`, not POC-era `add-repo` or `update-repo`.
96:- Allow linked repos or folders without repo-local `openspec/` state.
97:- Keep stable link names in shared workspace state and local paths in machine-local state.
98:- Make `doctor` show link names, resolved paths, repo-local specs paths when present, and suggested fixes.
99:
100:Defer:
101:
102:- Agent launch and workspace open behavior.
103:- Preferred-agent prompts.
104:- Owner or handoff metadata.
105:- Workspace change creation or target selection.
106:- Branches.
107:- Worktrees.
108:- Apply.
109:- Archive.
110:- Complex target lifecycle.
111:
112:Done when a user can set up a workspace, link repos or folders, list known workspaces, relink local paths, and run `doctor` to see exactly what OpenSpec can resolve.
113:
114:### 2. Workspace Open
115:
116:Next make the workspace openable in the way users expect.
117:
118:User goal:
119:
120:```text
121:Open this multi-repo planning context with my coding agent.
122:```
123:
124:Expected surface:
125:
126:```bash
127:openspec workspace open
128:openspec workspace open --agent codex
129:openspec workspace open --agent github-copilot
130:```
131:
132:Product behavior:
133:
134:- `workspace open` opens the coordination workspace plus linked repos or folders.
135:- Repo visibility is default.
136:- Change selection is optional focus, not the mechanism for repo access.
137:- `--agent` should be a one-session override by default. Persisting the preferred agent should require an explicit preference-setting action.
138:
139:For GitHub Copilot, generate or open a `.code-workspace` file with:
140:
141:```text
142:workspace folder
143:linked repo or folder A
144:linked repo or folder B
145:```
146:
147:For Claude and Codex, attach the linked repo or folder directories through the agent's supported mechanism.
148:
149:Defer:
150:
151:- `workspace open --change`.
152:- In-session upgrade flows.
153:- Per-change attachment restrictions.
154:
155:Done when opening a workspace gives the agent visibility into the coordination root and all linked repos or folders.
156:
157:### 3. Agent Guidance And Explore
158:
159:Then make exploration work.
160:
161:User goal:
162:
163:```text
164:Tell the agent a rough product goal and have it inspect the repos before creating a proposal.
165:```
166:
167:Expected user prompt:
168:
169:```text
170:Explore how we should make the OpenSpec docs available on the landing page.
171:Look across the linked repos or folders, but do not implement yet.
172:```
173:
174:Agent behavior:
175:
176:- Understand it is in workspace mode.
177:- Inspect linked repos or folders.
178:- Explain likely affected repos.
179:- Ask for clarification only when needed.
180:- Avoid implementation edits during explore.
181:
182:Build:
183:
184:- Workspace-level `AGENTS.md` guidance.
185:- Normal OpenSpec skills and commands in workspace sessions.
186:- Workspace-specific guidance layered on top of normal `/explore`, not replacing it.
187:
188:Defer:
189:
190:- Proposal artifact generation.
191:- Target confirmation commands.
192:- Apply context providers.
193:
194:Done when a user can open a workspace and run a useful cross-repo exploration without creating a dummy change.
195:
196:### 4. Proposal Creation
197:
198:Only after explore works, build proposal creation.
199:
200:User goal:
201:
202:```text
203:Now that we understand the scope, capture the plan.
204:```
205:
206:Expected user prompt:
207:
208:```text
209:Create a proposal for this change.
210:Target the repos that are actually affected.
211:```
212:
213:Preferred artifact shape:
214:
215:```text
216:changes/integrate-docs/
217:  proposal.md
218:  design.md
219:  tasks.md
220:  specs/
221:    openspec/
222:      docs-conventions/spec.md
223:    landing/
224:      docs-routing/spec.md
225:```
226:
227:Key workflow rule:
228:
229:```text
230:/explore may leave targets unknown.
231:/propose may discover targets.
232:/propose must confirm targets before saying ready for apply.
233:```
234:
235:Targets should be represented by the proposal artifacts themselves where possible. If there is `specs/landing/...`, then `landing` is in scope. Avoid a separate required `targets: [...]` metadata list as the active source of truth.
236:
237:Defer:
238:
239:- Repo-local materialization.
240:- Worktree selection.
241:- Multi-repo implementation.
242:- Archive.
243:
244:Done when a user can explore, then create a workspace proposal with repo-scoped specs and tasks.
245:
246:### 5. Status
247:
248:Before implementation, make status excellent.
249:
250:User goal:
251:
252:```text
253:Where are we, what repos are involved, and is this ready to implement?
254:```
255:
256:Expected surface:
257:
258:```bash
259:openspec status
260:openspec status --change integrate-docs
261:```
262:
263:Human output should answer:
264:
265:```text
266:Change: integrate-docs
267:Scope: openspec, landing
268:Proposal: present
269:Design: present
270:Tasks: present
271:Ready for apply: yes/no
272:```
273:
274:Status should also catch structural mistakes:
275:
276:- Unknown repo folder under `specs/`.
277:- Missing tasks.
278:- No confirmed affected repo.
279:- Linked repo or folder path missing.
280:
281:Done when the agent and user can trust status before applying.
282:
283:### 6. Apply One Repo Slice
284:
285:Only now build `/apply`.
286:
287:User goal:
288:
289:```text
290:Implement the planned slice for one repo.
291:```
292:
293:Expected user prompt:
294:
295:```text
296:/apply integrate-docs for landing
297:```
298:
299:Product contract:
300:
301:```text
302:/apply means implement.
303:```
304:
305:It does not mean:
306:
307:```text
308:copy planning files
309:materialize repo-local OpenSpec state
310:create the proposal files for the first time
311:```
312:
313:Agent behavior:
314:
315:1. Ask OpenSpec for apply context.
316:2. Read proposal, design, tasks, and relevant specs.
317:3. Confirm the target repo checkout.
318:4. Edit only that repo.
319:5. Update workspace tasks.
320:6. Run relevant checks.
321:
322:This likely wants a normalized context command internally, but that is supporting machinery:
323:
324:```json
325:{
326:  "mode": "workspace",
327:  "change": "integrate-docs",
328:  "target": "landing",
329:  "implementationRoot": "/repos/openspec-landing",
330:  "contextFiles": [
331:    "changes/integrate-docs/proposal.md",
332:    "changes/integrate-docs/design.md",
333:    "changes/integrate-docs/tasks.md",
334:    "changes/integrate-docs/specs/landing/docs-routing/spec.md"
335:  ],
336:  "allowedEditRoots": [
337:    "/repos/openspec-landing"
338:  ],
339:  "tasksFile": "changes/integrate-docs/tasks.md"
340:}
341:```
342:
343:Defer:
344:
345:- Applying multiple repos at once.
346:- Automatic branch creation.
347:- Worktree management.
348:- Repo-local OpenSpec mirroring.
349:
350:Done when one repo slice can be implemented from the central workspace plan.
351:
352:### 7. Verify
353:
354:Then build verification.
355:
356:User goal:
357:
358:```text
359:Check whether the implemented repo slice satisfies the plan.
360:```
361:
362:Expected prompt:
363:
364:```text
365:/verify integrate-docs for landing
366:```
367:
368:Behavior:
369:
370:- Read the same normalized context as `/apply`.
371:- Inspect the implementation checkout.
372:- Check tasks and specs for that repo.
373:- Run repo validation.
374:- Report gaps clearly.
375:
376:Default behavior should verify one repo slice. Whole-workspace verification can come later.
377:
378:Done when a user can verify one implemented repo slice against the central workspace plan.
379:
380:### 8. Archive
381:
382:Archive comes last in the first complete loop.
383:
384:User goal:
385:
386:```text
387:The change is done. Move it out of active planning.
388:```
389:
390:Expected prompt:
391:
392:```text
393:/archive integrate-docs
394:```
395:
396:Behavior:
397:
398:- Require all targeted repo slices to be complete or explicitly accepted.
399:- Archive the workspace change.
400:- Do not require repo-local planning copies unless OpenSpec later decides that repo-local archival matters.
401:
402:Done when a user can complete the full lifecycle:
403:
404:```text
405:workspace setup
406:  -> link repos or folders
407:  -> open
408:  -> explore
409:  -> propose
410:  -> apply repo A
411:  -> apply repo B
412:  -> verify
413:  -> archive
414:```
415:
416:## Implementation Discipline
417:
418:Build only the next user-visible step.
419:
420:The sequence should stay grounded in these questions:
421:
422:```text
423:1. Can I set up the workspace?
424:2. Can I see my linked repos or folders?
425:3. Can my agent explore them?
426:4. Can we capture a proposal?
427:5. Can status tell us if it is ready?
428:6. Can the agent implement one repo slice?
429:7. Can we verify it?
430:8. Can we archive it?
431:```
432:
433:Avoid starting with internal abstractions unless they are required for the next user-visible capability.
434:
435:Do not start with:
436:
437:- Target metadata machinery.
438:- Materialization.
439:- Adapter abstractions.
440:- Branch orchestration.
441:- Worktree orchestration.
442:- Multi-repo apply.
443:
444:Those may matter later, but they should not define the first reimplementation path.
445:
446:## Product Shape
447:
448:The workspace should feel like OpenSpec's normal workflow stretched across multiple repos, not a second product with its own lifecycle.
449:
450:The durable product model is:
451:
452:```text
453:workspace = durable planning home
454:links = repos or folders visible for planning
455:proposal = scoped planning commitment
456:repo slice = one affected repo or folder in the plan
457:branch/worktree = implementation checkout
458:/apply = implement one selected repo slice
459:```
460:
461:Keep the user journey simple:
462:
463:```text
464:Open the workspace.
465:Ask the agent to explore.
466:Create the proposal when scope is clear.
467:Implement one repo slice at a time.
468:Verify.
469:Archive.
470:```

79:
80:## Quick Start
81:
82:**Requires Node.js 20.19.0 or higher.**
83:
84:Install OpenSpec globally:
85:
86:```bash
87:npm install -g @fission-ai/openspec@latest
88:```
89:
90:Then navigate to your project directory and initialize:
91:
92:```bash
93:cd your-project
94:openspec init
95:```
96:
97:Now tell your AI: `/opsx:propose <what-you-want-to-build>`
98:
99:If you want the expanded workflow (`/opsx:new`, `/opsx:continue`, `/opsx:ff`, `/opsx:verify`, `/opsx:bulk-archive`, `/opsx:onboard`), select it with `openspec config profile` and apply with `openspec update`.
100:
101:> [!NOTE]
102:> Not sure if your tool is supported? [View the full list](docs/supported-tools.md) – we support 25+ tools and growing.
103:>
104:> Also works with pnpm, yarn, bun, and nix. [See installation options](docs/installation.md).
105:

118:## Community schemas
119:
120:Third-party schema bundles distributed via standalone repositories — these provide opinionated workflows that integrate OpenSpec with other tools, similar to how [github/spec-kit's community extension catalog](https://github.com/github/spec-kit/tree/main/extensions) handles tool integrations.
121:
122:→ **[Browse the catalog](docs/customization.md#community-schemas)** in the customization docs.
123:
124:
125:## Why OpenSpec?
126:
127:AI coding assistants are powerful but unpredictable when requirements live only in chat history. OpenSpec adds a lightweight spec layer so you agree on what to build before any code is written.
128:
129:- **Agree before you build** — human and AI align on specs before code gets written
130:- **Stay organized** — each change gets its own folder with proposal, specs, design, and tasks
131:- **Work fluidly** — update any artifact anytime, no rigid phase gates
132:- **Use your tools** — works with 20+ AI assistants via slash commands
133:
134:### How we compare
135:
136:**vs. [Spec Kit](https://github.com/github/spec-kit)** (GitHub) — Thorough but heavyweight. Rigid phase gates, lots of Markdown, Python setup. OpenSpec is lighter and lets you iterate freely.
137:
138:**vs. [Kiro](https://kiro.dev)** (AWS) — Powerful but you're locked into their IDE and limited to Claude models. OpenSpec works with the tools you already use.
139:
140:**vs. nothing** — AI coding without specs means vague prompts and unpredictable results. OpenSpec brings predictability without the ceremony.
141:
142:## Updating OpenSpec
143:
144:**Upgrade the package**
145:
146:```bash
147:npm install -g @fission-ai/openspec@latest
148:```
149:
150:**Refresh agent instructions**
151:
152:Run this inside each project to regenerate AI guidance and ensure the latest slash commands are active:
153:
154:```bash
155:openspec update
156:```
157:
158:## Usage Notes
159:
160:**Model selection**: OpenSpec works best with high-reasoning models. We recommend Opus 4.5 and GPT 5.2 for both planning and implementation.
161:
162:**Context hygiene**: OpenSpec benefits from a clean context window. Clear your context before starting implementation and maintain good context hygiene throughout your session.
163:
164:## Contributing
165:
166:**Small fixes** — Bug fixes, typo corrections, and minor improvements can be submitted directly as PRs.
167:
168:**Larger changes** — For new features, significant refactors, or architectural changes, please submit an OpenSpec change proposal first so we can align on intent and goals before implementation begins.
169:
170:When writing proposals, keep the OpenSpec philosophy in mind: we serve a wide variety of users across different coding agents, models, and use cases. Changes should work well for everyone.
171:
172:**AI-generated code is welcome** — as long as it's been tested and verified. PRs containing AI-generated code should mention the coding agent and model used (e.g., "Generated with Claude Code using claude-opus-4-5-20251101").
173:
174:### Development
175:
176:- Install dependencies: `pnpm install`
177:- Build: `pnpm run build`
178:- Test: `pnpm test`
179:- Develop CLI locally: `pnpm run dev` or `pnpm run dev:cli`
180:- Conventional commits (one-line): `type(scope): subject`
181:
182:## Other
183:
184:<details>
185:<summary><strong>Telemetry</strong></summary>
186:
187:OpenSpec collects anonymous usage stats.
188:
189:We collect only command names and version to understand usage patterns. No arguments, paths, content, or PII. Automatically disabled in CI.
190:
191:**Opt-out:** `export OPENSPEC_TELEMETRY=0` or `export DO_NOT_TRACK=1`
192:
193:</details>

Z:/repos/deps/openspec\README_OLD.md:387:### vs. Kiro.dev
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\README_OLD.md:388:OpenSpec groups every change for a feature in one folder (`openspec/changes/feature-name/`), making it easy to track related specs, tasks, and designs together. Kiro spreads updates across multiple spec folders, which can make feature tracking harder.

Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:6:### Requirement: AIToolOption skillsDir field
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:8:The `AIToolOption` interface SHALL include an optional `skillsDir` field for skill generation path configuration.
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:10:#### Scenario: Interface includes skillsDir field
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:13:- **THEN** it SHALL include a `skillsDir` field specifying the project-local base directory (e.g., `.claude`)
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:17:- **WHEN** generating skills for a tool with `skillsDir: '.claude'`
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:18:- **THEN** skills SHALL be written to `<projectRoot>/<skillsDir>/skills/`
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:19:- **AND** the `/skills` suffix is appended per Agent Skills specification
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:23:The `AI_TOOLS` array SHALL include `skillsDir` for tools that support the Agent Skills specification.
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:25:#### Scenario: Claude Code paths defined
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:28:- **THEN** `skillsDir` SHALL be `.claude`
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:30:#### Scenario: Cursor paths defined
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:33:- **THEN** `skillsDir` SHALL be `.cursor`
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:35:#### Scenario: Windsurf paths defined
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:38:- **THEN** `skillsDir` SHALL be `.windsurf`
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:43:- **THEN** `skillsDir` SHALL be `.kimi`
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:45:#### Scenario: Tools without skillsDir
Z:/repos/deps/openspec\openspec\specs\ai-tool-paths\spec.md:47:- **WHEN** a tool has no `skillsDir` defined

1:# Commands
2:
3:This is the reference for OpenSpec's slash commands. These commands are invoked in your AI coding assistant's chat interface (e.g., Claude Code, Cursor, Windsurf).
4:
5:For workflow patterns and when to use each command, see [Workflows](workflows.md). For CLI commands, see [CLI](cli.md).
6:
7:## Quick Reference
8:
9:### Default Quick Path (`core` profile)
10:
11:| Command | Purpose |
12:|---------|---------|
13:| `/opsx:propose` | Create a change and generate planning artifacts in one step |
14:| `/opsx:explore` | Think through ideas before committing to a change |
15:| `/opsx:apply` | Implement tasks from the change |
16:| `/opsx:sync` | Merge delta specs into main specs |
17:| `/opsx:archive` | Archive a completed change |
18:
19:### Expanded Workflow Commands (custom workflow selection)
20:
21:| Command | Purpose |
22:|---------|---------|
23:| `/opsx:new` | Start a new change scaffold |
24:| `/opsx:continue` | Create the next artifact based on dependencies |
25:| `/opsx:ff` | Fast-forward: create all planning artifacts at once |
26:| `/opsx:verify` | Validate implementation matches artifacts |
27:| `/opsx:bulk-archive` | Archive multiple changes at once |
28:| `/opsx:onboard` | Guided tutorial through the complete workflow |
29:
30:The default global profile is `core`. To enable expanded workflow commands, run `openspec config profile`, select workflows, then run `openspec update` in your project.
31:
32:---
33:
34:## Command Reference
35:
36:### `/opsx:propose`
37:
38:Create a new change and generate planning artifacts in one step. This is the default start command in the `core` profile.
39:
40:**Syntax:**
41:```text
42:/opsx:propose [change-name-or-description]
43:```
44:
45:**Arguments:**
46:| Argument | Required | Description |
47:|----------|----------|-------------|
48:| `change-name-or-description` | No | Kebab-case name or plain-language change description |
49:
50:**What it does:**
51:- Creates `openspec/changes/<change-name>/`
52:- Generates artifacts needed before implementation (for `spec-driven`: proposal, specs, design, tasks)
53:- Stops when the change is ready for `/opsx:apply`
54:
55:**Example:**
56:```text
57:You: /opsx:propose add-dark-mode
58:
59:AI:  Created openspec/changes/add-dark-mode/
60:     ✓ proposal.md
61:     ✓ specs/ui/spec.md
62:     ✓ design.md
63:     ✓ tasks.md
64:     Ready for implementation. Run /opsx:apply.
65:```
66:
67:**Tips:**
68:- Use this for the fastest end-to-end path
69:- If you want step-by-step artifact control, enable expanded workflows and use `/opsx:new` + `/opsx:continue`
70:
71:---
72:
73:### `/opsx:explore`
74:
75:Think through ideas, investigate problems, and clarify requirements before committing to a change.
76:
77:**Syntax:**
78:```
79:/opsx:explore [topic]
80:```
81:
82:**Arguments:**
83:| Argument | Required | Description |
84:|----------|----------|-------------|
85:| `topic` | No | What you want to explore or investigate |
86:
87:**What it does:**
88:- Opens an exploratory conversation with no structure required
89:- Investigates the codebase to answer questions
90:- Compares options and approaches
91:- Creates visual diagrams to clarify thinking
92:- Can transition to `/opsx:propose` (default) or `/opsx:new` (expanded workflow) when insights crystallize
93:
94:**Example:**
95:```text
96:You: /opsx:explore
97:
98:AI:  What would you like to explore?
99:
100:You: How should we handle authentication for the mobile app?
101:
102:AI:  Let me investigate your current auth setup...
103:     [Analyzes codebase]
104:
105:     I see you're using session-based auth for the web app.
106:     For mobile, we have three main options:
107:
108:     1. JWT tokens (stateless, works offline)
109:     2. OAuth2 (if you need third-party login)
110:     3. Extend current sessions (simpler, but requires connectivity)
111:
112:     Your API already has CORS configured. Which direction interests you?
113:
114:You: Let's go with JWT. Can we start a change for that?
115:
116:AI:  Ready when you are. Run /opsx:propose add-jwt-auth to begin.
117:```
118:
119:**Tips:**
120:- Use when requirements are unclear or you need to investigate
121:- No artifacts are created during exploration
122:- Good for comparing multiple approaches before deciding
123:- Can read files and search the codebase
124:
125:---
126:
127:### `/opsx:new`
128:
129:Start a new change scaffold. Creates the change folder and waits for you to generate artifacts with `/opsx:continue` or `/opsx:ff`.
130:
131:This command is part of the expanded workflow set (not included in the default `core` profile).
132:
133:**Syntax:**
134:```
135:/opsx:new [change-name] [--schema <schema-name>]
136:```
137:
138:**Arguments:**
139:| Argument | Required | Description |
140:|----------|----------|-------------|
141:| `change-name` | No | Name for the change folder (prompted if not provided) |
142:| `--schema` | No | Workflow schema to use (default: from config or `spec-driven`) |
143:
144:**What it does:**
145:- Creates `openspec/changes/<change-name>/` directory
146:- Creates `.openspec.yaml` metadata file in the change folder
147:- Shows the first artifact template ready for creation
148:- Prompts for change name and schema if not provided
149:
150:**What it creates:**
151:```
152:openspec/changes/<change-name>/
153:└── .openspec.yaml    # Change metadata (schema, created date)
154:```
155:
156:**Example:**
157:```
158:You: /opsx:new add-dark-mode
159:
160:AI:  Created openspec/changes/add-dark-mode/
161:     Schema: spec-driven
162:
163:     Ready to create: proposal
164:     Use /opsx:continue to create it, or /opsx:ff to create all artifacts.
165:```
166:
167:**Tips:**
168:- Use descriptive names: `add-feature`, `fix-bug`, `refactor-module`
169:- Avoid generic names like `update`, `changes`, `wip`
170:- Schema can also be set in project config (`openspec/config.yaml`)
171:
172:---
173:
174:### `/opsx:continue`
175:
176:Create the next artifact in the dependency chain. Creates one artifact at a time for incremental progress.
177:
178:**Syntax:**
179:```
180:/opsx:continue [change-name]
181:```
182:
183:**Arguments:**
184:| Argument | Required | Description |
185:|----------|----------|-------------|
186:| `change-name` | No | Which change to continue (inferred from context if not provided) |
187:
188:**What it does:**
189:- Queries the artifact dependency graph
190:- Shows which artifacts are ready vs blocked
191:- Creates the first ready artifact
192:- Reads dependency files for context
193:- Shows what becomes available after creation
194:
195:**Example:**
196:```
197:You: /opsx:continue
198:
199:AI:  Change: add-dark-mode
200:
201:     Artifact status:
202:     ✓ proposal    (done)
203:     ◆ specs       (ready)
204:     ◆ design      (ready)
205:     ○ tasks       (blocked - needs: specs)
206:
207:     Creating specs...
208:
209:     [Reads proposal.md, creates specs based on requirements]
210:
211:     ✓ Created openspec/changes/add-dark-mode/specs/ui/spec.md
212:
213:     Now available: tasks
214:     Run /opsx:continue to create the next artifact.
215:```
216:
217:**Tips:**
218:- Use when you want to review each artifact before proceeding
219:- Good for complex changes where you want control
220:- Multiple artifacts may become ready simultaneously
221:- You can edit created artifacts before continuing
222:
223:---
224:
225:### `/opsx:ff`
226:
227:Fast-forward through artifact creation. Creates all planning artifacts at once.
228:
229:**Syntax:**
230:```
231:/opsx:ff [change-name]
232:```
233:
234:**Arguments:**
235:| Argument | Required | Description |
236:|----------|----------|-------------|
237:| `change-name` | No | Which change to fast-forward (inferred from context if not provided) |
238:
239:**What it does:**
240:- Creates all artifacts in dependency order
241:- Tracks progress via todo list
242:- Stops when all `apply-required` artifacts are complete
243:- Reads each dependency before creating the next artifact
244:
245:**Example:**
246:```
247:You: /opsx:ff add-dark-mode
248:
249:AI:  Fast-forwarding add-dark-mode...
250:
251:     ✓ Creating proposal.md
252:     ✓ Creating specs/ui/spec.md
253:     ✓ Creating design.md
254:     ✓ Creating tasks.md
255:
256:     All planning artifacts complete!
257:     Ready for implementation. Run /opsx:apply to begin.
258:```
259:
260:**Tips:**
261:- Use when you have a clear picture of what you're building
262:- Faster than `/opsx:continue` for straightforward changes
263:- You can still edit artifacts afterward
264:- Good for small to medium features
265:
266:---
267:
268:### `/opsx:apply`
269:
270:Implement tasks from the change. Works through the task list, writing code and checking off items.
271:
272:**Syntax:**
273:```
274:/opsx:apply [change-name]
275:```
276:
277:**Arguments:**
278:| Argument | Required | Description |
279:|----------|----------|-------------|
280:| `change-name` | No | Which change to implement (inferred from context if not provided) |
281:
282:**What it does:**
283:- Reads `tasks.md` and identifies incomplete tasks
284:- Works through tasks one by one
285:- Writes code, creates files, runs tests as needed
286:- Marks tasks complete with checkboxes `[x]`
287:
288:**Example:**
289:```
290:You: /opsx:apply
291:
292:AI:  Implementing add-dark-mode...
293:
294:     Reading tasks.md:
295:     - [ ] 1.1 Create ThemeContext
296:     - [ ] 1.2 Add CSS custom properties
297:     - [ ] 1.3 Implement localStorage persistence
298:
299:     Working on 1.1: Create ThemeContext...
300:     [Creates src/context/ThemeContext.tsx]
301:
302:     ✓ 1.1 Complete
303:
304:     Working on 1.2: Add CSS custom properties...
305:     [Updates src/styles/globals.css]
306:
307:     ✓ 1.2 Complete
308:     ...
309:```
310:
311:**Tips:**
312:- Can resume where you left off if interrupted
313:- Use for parallel changes by specifying the change name
314:- Completion state is tracked in `tasks.md` checkboxes
315:
316:---
317:
318:### `/opsx:verify`
319:
320:Validate that implementation matches your change artifacts. Checks completeness, correctness, and coherence.
321:
322:**Syntax:**
323:```
324:/opsx:verify [change-name]
325:```
326:
327:**Arguments:**
328:| Argument | Required | Description |
329:|----------|----------|-------------|
330:| `change-name` | No | Which change to verify (inferred from context if not provided) |
331:
332:**What it does:**
333:- Checks three dimensions of implementation quality
334:- Searches codebase for implementation evidence
335:- Reports issues categorized as CRITICAL, WARNING, or SUGGESTION
336:- Does not block archive, but surfaces issues
337:
338:**Verification dimensions:**
339:
340:| Dimension | What it validates |
341:|-----------|-------------------|
342:| **Completeness** | All tasks done, all requirements implemented, scenarios covered |
343:| **Correctness** | Implementation matches spec intent, edge cases handled |
344:| **Coherence** | Design decisions reflected in code, patterns consistent |
345:
346:**Example:**
347:```
348:You: /opsx:verify
349:
350:AI:  Verifying add-dark-mode...
351:
352:     COMPLETENESS
353:     ✓ All 8 tasks in tasks.md are checked
354:     ✓ All requirements in specs have corresponding code
355:     ⚠ Scenario "System preference detection" has no test coverage
356:
357:     CORRECTNESS
358:     ✓ Implementation matches spec intent
359:     ✓ Edge cases from scenarios are handled
360:     ✓ Error states match spec definitions
361:
362:     COHERENCE
363:     ✓ Design decisions reflected in code structure
364:     ✓ Naming conventions consistent with design.md
365:     ⚠ Design mentions "CSS variables" but implementation uses Tailwind classes
366:
367:     SUMMARY
368:     ─────────────────────────────
369:     Critical issues: 0
370:     Warnings: 2
371:     Ready to archive: Yes (with warnings)
372:
373:     Recommendations:
374:     1. Add test for system preference detection
375:     2. Update design.md to reflect Tailwind usage, or refactor to use CSS variables
376:```
377:
378:**Tips:**
379:- Run before archiving to catch mismatches early
380:- Warnings don't block archive but indicate potential issues
381:- Good for reviewing AI's work before committing
382:- Can reveal drift between artifacts and implementation
383:
384:---
385:
386:### `/opsx:sync`
387:
388:**Optional command.** Merge delta specs from a change into main specs. Archive will prompt to sync if needed, so you typically don't need to run this manually.
389:
390:**Syntax:**
391:```
392:/opsx:sync [change-name]
393:```
394:
395:**Arguments:**
396:| Argument | Required | Description |
397:|----------|----------|-------------|
398:| `change-name` | No | Which change to sync (inferred from context if not provided) |
399:
400:**What it does:**
401:- Reads delta specs from change folder
402:- Parses ADDED/MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED sections
403:- Merges changes into main `openspec/specs/` directory
404:- Preserves existing content not mentioned in delta
405:- Does not archive the change (remains active)
406:
407:**Example:**
408:```text
409:You: /opsx:sync
410:
411:AI:  Syncing add-dark-mode delta specs...
412:
413:     Reading: openspec/changes/add-dark-mode/specs/ui/spec.md
414:     Target:  openspec/specs/ui/spec.md
415:
416:     Changes to apply:
417:     ✓ ADDED: Theme Selection requirement (2 scenarios)
418:     ✓ ADDED: Accessibility requirement (1 scenario)
419:
420:     Merging...
421:     ✓ openspec/specs/ui/spec.md updated
422:
423:     Change remains active. Run /opsx:archive when ready to complete.
424:```
425:
426:**When to use manually:**
427:
428:| Scenario | Use sync? |
429:|----------|-----------|
430:| Long-running change, want specs in main before archiving | Yes |
431:| Multiple parallel changes need the updated base specs | Yes |
432:| Want to preview/review the merge separately | Yes |
433:| Quick change, going straight to archive | No (archive handles it) |
434:
435:**Tips:**
436:- Sync is intelligent, not copy-paste
437:- Can add scenarios to existing requirements without duplicating
438:- Change stays active after sync (not archived)
439:- Most users will never need to call this directly—archive prompts if needed
440:
441:---
442:
443:### `/opsx:archive`
444:
445:Archive a completed change. Finalizes the change and moves it to the archive folder.
446:
447:**Syntax:**
448:```
449:/opsx:archive [change-name]
450:```
451:
452:**Arguments:**
453:| Argument | Required | Description |
454:|----------|----------|-------------|
455:| `change-name` | No | Which change to archive (inferred from context if not provided) |
456:
457:**What it does:**
458:- Checks artifact completion status
459:- Checks task completion (warns if incomplete)
460:- Offers to sync delta specs if not already synced
461:- Moves change folder to `openspec/changes/archive/YYYY-MM-DD-<name>/`
462:- Preserves all artifacts for audit trail
463:
464:**Example:**
465:```text
466:You: /opsx:archive
467:
468:AI:  Archiving add-dark-mode...
469:
470:     Artifact status:
471:     ✓ proposal.md exists
472:     ✓ specs/ exists
473:     ✓ design.md exists
474:     ✓ tasks.md exists (8/8 tasks complete)
475:
476:     Delta specs: Not yet synced
477:     → Sync now? (recommended)
478:
479:You: Yes
480:
481:AI:  ✓ Synced specs to openspec/specs/ui/spec.md
482:     ✓ Moved to openspec/changes/archive/2025-01-24-add-dark-mode/
483:
484:     Change archived successfully.
485:```
486:
487:**Tips:**
488:- Archive won't block on incomplete tasks, but will warn
489:- Delta specs can be synced during archive or beforehand
490:- Archived changes are preserved for history
491:- Use `/opsx:verify` first to catch issues
492:
493:---
494:
495:### `/opsx:bulk-archive`
496:
497:Archive multiple completed changes at once. Handles spec conflicts between changes.
498:
499:**Syntax:**
500:```
501:/opsx:bulk-archive [change-names...]
502:```
503:
504:**Arguments:**
505:| Argument | Required | Description |
506:|----------|----------|-------------|
507:| `change-names` | No | Specific changes to archive (prompts to select if not provided) |
508:
509:**What it does:**
510:- Lists all completed changes
511:- Validates each change before archiving
512:- Detects spec conflicts across changes
513:- Resolves conflicts by checking what's actually implemented
514:- Archives in chronological order
515:
516:**Example:**
517:```
518:You: /opsx:bulk-archive
519:
520:AI:  Found 3 completed changes:
521:     - add-dark-mode (8/8 tasks complete)
522:     - fix-login-bug (3/3 tasks complete)
523:     - update-footer (2/2 tasks complete)
524:
525:     Checking for spec conflicts...
526:     ⚠ add-dark-mode and update-footer both touch specs/ui/
527:
528:     Inspecting codebase to resolve...
529:     Both changes are implemented. Will merge in chronological order:
530:     1. add-dark-mode (created Jan 20)
531:     2. update-footer (created Jan 22)
532:
533:     Archive all 3 changes?
534:
535:You: Yes
536:
537:AI:  ✓ Archived add-dark-mode
538:     ✓ Archived fix-login-bug
539:     ✓ Archived update-footer
540:     Specs merged: add-dark-mode → update-footer
541:```
542:
543:**Tips:**
544:- Good for parallel work streams
545:- Conflict resolution is agentic (checks codebase)
546:- Changes are archived in order of creation
547:- Prompts before overwriting spec content
548:
549:---
550:
551:### `/opsx:onboard`
552:
553:Guided onboarding through the complete OpenSpec workflow. An interactive tutorial using your actual codebase.
554:
555:**Syntax:**
556:```
557:/opsx:onboard
558:```
559:
560:**What it does:**
561:- Walks through a complete workflow cycle with narration
562:- Scans your codebase for real improvement opportunities
563:- Creates an actual change with real artifacts
564:- Implements actual work (small, safe changes)
565:- Archives the completed change
566:- Explains each step as it happens
567:
568:**Phases:**
569:1. Welcome and codebase analysis
570:2. Finding an improvement opportunity
571:3. Creating a change (`/opsx:new`)
572:4. Writing the proposal
573:5. Creating specs
574:6. Writing the design
575:7. Creating tasks
576:8. Implementing tasks (`/opsx:apply`)
577:9. Verifying implementation
578:10. Archiving the change
579:11. Summary and next steps
580:
581:**Example:**
582:```
583:You: /opsx:onboard
584:
585:AI:  Welcome to OpenSpec!
586:
587:     I'll walk you through the complete workflow using your actual codebase.
588:     We'll find something small to improve, create a proper change for it,
589:     implement it, and archive it.
590:
591:     Let me scan your codebase for opportunities...
592:
593:     [Analyzes codebase]
594:
595:     I found a few things we could work on:
596:     1. Add input validation to the contact form
597:     2. Improve error messages in the auth flow
598:     3. Add loading states to async buttons
599:
600:     Which interests you? (or suggest something else)
601:```
602:
603:**Tips:**
604:- Best for new users learning the workflow
605:- Uses real code, not toy examples
606:- Creates a real change you can keep or discard
607:- Takes 15-30 minutes to complete
608:
609:---
610:
611:## Command Syntax by AI Tool
612:
613:Different AI tools use slightly different command syntax. Use the format that matches your tool:
614:
615:| Tool | Syntax Example |
616:|------|----------------|
617:| Claude Code | `/opsx:propose`, `/opsx:apply` |
618:| Cursor | `/opsx-propose`, `/opsx-apply` |
619:| Windsurf | `/opsx-propose`, `/opsx-apply` |
620:| Copilot (IDE) | `/opsx-propose`, `/opsx-apply` |
621:| Kimi CLI | Skill-based invocations such as `/skill:openspec-propose`, `/skill:openspec-apply-change` (no generated `opsx-*` command files) |
622:| Trae | Skill-based invocations such as `/openspec-propose`, `/openspec-apply-change` (no generated `opsx-*` command files) |
623:
624:The intent is the same across tools, but how commands are surfaced can differ by integration.
625:
626:> **Note:** GitHub Copilot commands (`.github/prompts/*.prompt.md`) are only available in IDE extensions (VS Code, JetBrains, Visual Studio). GitHub Copilot CLI does not currently support custom prompt files — see [Supported Tools](supported-tools.md) for details and workarounds.
627:
628:---
629:
630:## Legacy Commands
631:
632:These commands use the older "all-at-once" workflow. They still work but OPSX commands are recommended.
633:
634:| Command | What it does |
635:|---------|--------------|
636:| `/openspec:proposal` | Create all artifacts at once (proposal, specs, design, tasks) |
637:| `/openspec:apply` | Implement the change |
638:| `/openspec:archive` | Archive the change |
639:
640:**When to use legacy commands:**
641:- Existing projects using the old workflow
642:- Simple changes where you don't need incremental artifact creation
643:- Preference for the all-or-nothing approach
644:
645:**Migrating to OPSX:**
646:Legacy changes can be continued with OPSX commands. The artifact structure is compatible.
647:
648:---
649:
650:## Troubleshooting
651:
652:### "Change not found"
653:
654:The command couldn't identify which change to work on.
655:
656:**Solutions:**
657:- Specify the change name explicitly: `/opsx:apply add-dark-mode`
658:- Check that the change folder exists: `openspec list`
659:- Verify you're in the right project directory
660:
661:### "No artifacts ready"
662:
663:All artifacts are either complete or blocked by missing dependencies.
664:
665:**Solutions:**
666:- Run `openspec status --change <name>` to see what's blocking
667:- Check if required artifacts exist
668:- Create missing dependency artifacts first
669:
670:### "Schema not found"
671:
672:The specified schema doesn't exist.
673:
674:**Solutions:**
675:- List available schemas: `openspec schemas`
676:- Check spelling of schema name
677:- Create the schema if it's custom: `openspec schema init <name>`
678:
679:### Commands not recognized
680:
681:The AI tool doesn't recognize OpenSpec commands.
682:
683:**Solutions:**
684:- Ensure OpenSpec is initialized: `openspec init`
685:- Regenerate skills: `openspec update`
686:- Check that `.claude/skills/` directory exists (for Claude Code)
687:- Restart your AI tool to pick up new skills
688:
689:### Artifacts not generating properly
690:
691:The AI creates incomplete or incorrect artifacts.
692:
693:**Solutions:**
694:- Add project context in `openspec/config.yaml`
695:- Add per-artifact rules for specific guidance
696:- Provide more detail in your change description
697:- Use `/opsx:continue` instead of `/opsx:ff` for more control
698:

1:# Supported Tools
2:
3:OpenSpec works with many AI coding assistants. When you run `openspec init`, OpenSpec configures selected tools using your active profile/workflow selection and delivery mode.
4:
5:## How It Works
6:
7:For each selected tool, OpenSpec can install:
8:
9:1. **Skills** (if delivery includes skills): `.../skills/openspec-*/SKILL.md`
10:2. **Commands** (if delivery includes commands): tool-specific `opsx-*` command files
11:
12:By default, OpenSpec uses the `core` profile, which includes:
13:- `propose`
14:- `explore`
15:- `apply`
16:- `sync`
17:- `archive`
18:
19:You can enable expanded workflows (`new`, `continue`, `ff`, `verify`, `bulk-archive`, `onboard`) via `openspec config profile`, then run `openspec update`.
20:

59:## Non-Interactive Setup
60:
61:For CI/CD or scripted setup, use `--tools` (and optionally `--profile`):
62:
63:```bash
64:# Configure specific tools
65:openspec init --tools claude,cursor
66:
67:# Configure all supported tools
68:openspec init --tools all
69:
70:# Skip tool configuration
71:openspec init --tools none
72:
73:# Override profile for this init run
74:openspec init --profile core
75:```
76:
77:**Available tool IDs (`--tools`):** `amazon-q`, `antigravity`, `auggie`, `bob`, `claude`, `cline`, `codex`, `forgecode`, `codebuddy`, `continue`, `costrict`, `crush`, `cursor`, `factory`, `gemini`, `github-copilot`, `iflow`, `junie`, `kilocode`, `kimi`, `kiro`, `opencode`, `pi`, `qoder`, `lingma`, `qwen`, `roocode`, `trae`, `windsurf`
78:
79:## Workflow-Dependent Installation
80:
81:OpenSpec installs workflow artifacts based on selected workflows:
82:
83:- **Core profile (default):** `propose`, `explore`, `apply`, `sync`, `archive`
84:- **Custom selection:** any subset of all workflow IDs:
85:  `propose`, `explore`, `new`, `continue`, `apply`, `ff`, `sync`, `archive`, `bulk-archive`, `verify`, `onboard`
86:
87:In other words, skill/command counts are profile-dependent and delivery-dependent, not fixed.
88:
89:## Generated Skill Names
90:
91:When selected by profile/workflow config, OpenSpec generates these skills:
92:
93:- `openspec-propose`
94:- `openspec-explore`
95:- `openspec-new-change`
96:- `openspec-continue-change`
97:- `openspec-apply-change`
98:- `openspec-ff-change`
99:- `openspec-sync-specs`
100:- `openspec-archive-change`
101:- `openspec-bulk-archive-change`
102:- `openspec-verify-change`
103:- `openspec-onboard`
104:
105:See [Commands](commands.md) for command behavior and [CLI](cli.md) for `init`/`update` options.

32:
33:# OpenSpec
34:
35:OpenSpec aligns humans and AI coding assistants with spec-driven development so you agree on what to build before any code is written. **No API keys required.**
36:
37:## Why OpenSpec?
38:
39:AI coding assistants are powerful but unpredictable when requirements live in chat history. OpenSpec adds a lightweight specification workflow that locks intent before implementation, giving you deterministic, reviewable outputs.
40:
41:Key outcomes:
42:- Human and AI stakeholders agree on specs before work begins.
43:- Structured change folders (proposals, tasks, and spec updates) keep scope explicit and auditable.
44:- Shared visibility into what's proposed, active, or archived.
45:- Works with the AI tools you already use: custom slash commands where supported, context rules everywhere else.
46:
47:## How OpenSpec compares (at a glance)
48:
49:- **Lightweight**: simple workflow, no API keys, minimal setup.
50:- **Brownfield-first**: works great beyond 0→1. OpenSpec separates the source of truth from proposals: `openspec/specs/` (current truth) and `openspec/changes/` (proposed updates). This keeps diffs explicit and manageable across features.
51:- **Change tracking**: proposals, tasks, and spec deltas live together; archiving merges the approved updates back into specs.
52:- **Compared to spec-kit & Kiro**: those shine for brand-new features (0→1). OpenSpec also excels when modifying existing behavior (1→n), especially when updates span multiple specs.
53:
54:See the full comparison in [How OpenSpec Compares](#how-openspec-compares).
55:
56:## How It Works
57:
58:```
59:┌────────────────────┐
60:│ Draft Change       │
61:│ Proposal           │
62:└────────┬───────────┘
63:         │ share intent with your AI
64:         ▼
65:┌────────────────────┐
66:│ Review & Align     │
67:│ (edit specs/tasks) │◀──── feedback loop ──────┐
68:└────────┬───────────┘                          │
69:         │ approved plan                        │
70:         ▼                                      │
71:┌────────────────────┐                          │
72:│ Implement Tasks    │──────────────────────────┘
73:│ (AI writes code)   │
74:└────────┬───────────┘
75:         │ ship the change
76:         ▼
77:┌────────────────────┐
78:│ Archive & Update   │
79:│ Specs (source)     │
80:└────────────────────┘
81:
82:1. Draft a change proposal that captures the spec updates you want.
83:2. Review the proposal with your AI assistant until everyone agrees.
84:3. Implement tasks that reference the agreed specs.
85:4. Archive the change to merge the approved updates back into the source-of-truth specs.
86:```
87:
88:## Getting Started
89:
90:### Supported AI Tools
91:
92:<details>
93:<summary><strong>Native Slash Commands</strong> (click to expand)</summary>
94:
95:These tools have built-in OpenSpec commands. Select the OpenSpec integration when prompted.
96:
97:| Tool | Commands |
98:|------|----------|
99:| **Amazon Q Developer** | `@openspec-proposal`, `@openspec-apply`, `@openspec-archive` (`.amazonq/prompts/`) |
100:| **Antigravity** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.agent/workflows/`) |
101:| **Auggie (Augment CLI)** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.augment/commands/`) |
102:| **Claude Code** | `/openspec:proposal`, `/openspec:apply`, `/openspec:archive` |
103:| **Cline** | Workflows in `.clinerules/workflows/` directory (`.clinerules/workflows/openspec-*.md`) |
104:| **CodeBuddy Code (CLI)** | `/openspec:proposal`, `/openspec:apply`, `/openspec:archive` (`.codebuddy/commands/`) — see [docs](https://www.codebuddy.ai/cli) |
105:| **Codex** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (global: `~/.codex/prompts`, auto-installed) |
106:| **Continue** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.continue/prompts/`) |
107:| **CoStrict** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.cospec/openspec/commands/`) — see [docs](https://costrict.ai)|
108:| **Crush** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.crush/commands/openspec/`) |
109:| **Cursor** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` |
110:| **Factory Droid** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.factory/commands/`) |
111:| **Gemini CLI** | `/openspec:proposal`, `/openspec:apply`, `/openspec:archive` (`.gemini/commands/openspec/`) |
112:| **GitHub Copilot** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.github/prompts/`) |
113:| **iFlow (iflow-cli)** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.iflow/commands/`) |
114:| **Kilo Code** | `/openspec-proposal.md`, `/openspec-apply.md`, `/openspec-archive.md` (`.kilocode/workflows/`) |
115:| **OpenCode** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` |
116:| **Qoder** | `/openspec:proposal`, `/openspec:apply`, `/openspec:archive` (`.qoder/commands/openspec/`) — see [docs](https://qoder.com) |
117:| **Qwen Code** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.qwen/commands/`) |
118:| **RooCode** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.roo/commands/`) |
119:| **Windsurf** | `/openspec-proposal`, `/openspec-apply`, `/openspec-archive` (`.windsurf/workflows/`) |
120:
121:Kilo Code discovers team workflows automatically. Save the generated files under `.kilocode/workflows/` and trigger them from the command palette with `/openspec-proposal.md`, `/openspec-apply.md`, or `/openspec-archive.md`.

126:<summary><strong>AGENTS.md Compatible</strong> (click to expand)</summary>
127:
128:These tools automatically read workflow instructions from `openspec/AGENTS.md`. Ask them to follow the OpenSpec workflow if they need a reminder. Learn more about the [AGENTS.md convention](https://agents.md/).

135:
136:### Install & Initialize
137:
138:#### Prerequisites
139:- **Node.js >= 20.19.0** - Check your version with `node --version`
140:
141:#### Step 1: Install the CLI globally
142:
143:**Option A: Using npm**
144:
145:```bash
146:npm install -g @fission-ai/openspec@latest
147:```
148:
149:Verify installation:
150:```bash
151:openspec --version
152:```
153:
154:**Option B: Using Nix (NixOS and Nix package manager)**
155:
156:Run OpenSpec directly without installation:
157:```bash
158:nix run github:Fission-AI/OpenSpec -- init
159:```
160:
161:Or install to your profile:
162:```bash
163:nix profile install github:Fission-AI/OpenSpec
164:```
165:
166:Or add to your development environment in `flake.nix`:
167:```nix
168:{
169:  inputs = {
170:    nixpkgs.url = "github:NixOS/nixpkgs/nixos-unstable";
171:    openspec.url = "github:Fission-AI/OpenSpec";
172:  };
173:
174:  outputs = { nixpkgs, openspec, ... }: {
175:    devShells.x86_64-linux.default = nixpkgs.legacyPackages.x86_64-linux.mkShell {
176:      buildInputs = [ openspec.packages.x86_64-linux.default ];
177:    };
178:  };
179:}
180:```
181:
182:Verify installation:
183:```bash
184:openspec --version
185:```
186:
187:#### Step 2: Initialize OpenSpec in your project
188:
189:Navigate to your project directory:
190:```bash
191:cd my-project
192:```
193:
194:Run the initialization:
195:```bash
196:openspec init
197:```
198:
199:**What happens during initialization:**
200:- You'll be prompted to pick any natively supported AI tools (Claude Code, CodeBuddy, Cursor, OpenCode, Qoder,etc.); other assistants always rely on the shared `AGENTS.md` stub
201:- OpenSpec automatically configures slash commands for the tools you choose and always writes a managed `AGENTS.md` hand-off at the project root
202:- A new `openspec/` directory structure is created in your project
203:
204:**After setup:**
205:- Primary AI tools can trigger `/openspec` workflows without additional configuration
206:- Run `openspec list` to verify the setup and view any active changes
207:- If your coding assistant doesn't surface the new slash commands right away, restart it. Slash commands are loaded at startup,
208:  so a fresh launch ensures they appear
209:
210:### Optional: Populate Project Context
211:
212:After `openspec init` completes, you'll receive a suggested prompt to help populate your project context:
213:
214:```text
215:Populate your project context:
216:"Please read openspec/project.md and help me fill it out with details about my project, tech stack, and conventions"
217:```
218:
219:Use `openspec/project.md` to define project-level conventions, standards, architectural patterns, and other guidelines that should be followed across all changes.
220:
221:### Create Your First Change
222:
223:Here's a real example showing the complete OpenSpec workflow. This works with any AI tool. Those with native slash commands will recognize the shortcuts automatically.
224:
225:#### 1. Draft the Proposal
226:Start by asking your AI to create a change proposal:
227:
228:```text
229:You: Create an OpenSpec change proposal for adding profile search filters by role and team
230:     (Shortcut for tools with slash commands: /openspec:proposal Add profile search filters)
231:
232:AI:  I'll create an OpenSpec change proposal for profile filters.
233:     *Scaffolds openspec/changes/add-profile-filters/ with proposal.md, tasks.md, spec deltas.*
234:```
235:
236:#### 2. Verify & Review
237:Check that the change was created correctly and review the proposal:
238:
239:```bash
240:$ openspec list                             # Confirm the change folder exists
241:$ openspec validate add-profile-filters     # Validate spec formatting
242:$ openspec show add-profile-filters         # Review proposal, tasks, and spec delta
243:```
244:
245:#### 3. Refine the Specs
246:Iterate on the specifications until they match your needs:
247:
248:```text
249:You: Can you add acceptance criteria for the role and team filters?
250:
251:AI:  I'll update the spec delta with scenarios for role and team filters.
252:     *Edits openspec/changes/add-profile-filters/specs/profile/spec.md and tasks.md.*
253:```
254:
255:#### 4. Implement the Change
256:Once specs look good, start implementation:
257:
258:```text
259:You: The specs look good. Let's implement this change.
260:     (Shortcut for tools with slash commands: /openspec:apply add-profile-filters)
261:
262:AI:  I'll work through the tasks in the add-profile-filters change.
263:     *Implements tasks from openspec/changes/add-profile-filters/tasks.md*
264:     *Marks tasks complete: Task 1.1 ✓, Task 1.2 ✓, Task 2.1 ✓...*
265:```
266:
267:#### 5. Archive the Completed Change
268:After implementation is complete, archive the change:
269:
270:```text
271:AI:  All tasks are complete. The implementation is ready.
272:
273:You: Please archive the change
274:     (Shortcut for tools with slash commands: /openspec:archive add-profile-filters)
275:
276:AI:  I'll archive the add-profile-filters change.
277:    *Runs: openspec archive add-profile-filters --yes*
278:     ✓ Change archived successfully. Specs updated. Ready for the next feature!
279:```
280:
281:Or run the command yourself in terminal:
282:```bash
283:$ openspec archive add-profile-filters --yes  # Archive the completed change without prompts
284:```
285:
286:**Note:** Tools with native slash commands (Claude Code, CodeBuddy, Cursor, Codex, Qoder, RooCode) can use the shortcuts shown. All other tools work with natural language requests to "create an OpenSpec proposal", "apply the OpenSpec change", or "archive the change".
287:
288:## Command Reference
289:
290:```bash
291:openspec list               # View active change folders
292:openspec view               # Interactive dashboard of specs and changes
293:openspec show <change>      # Display change details (proposal, tasks, spec updates)
294:openspec validate <change>  # Check spec formatting and structure
295:openspec archive <change> [--yes|-y]   # Move a completed change into archive/ (non-interactive with --yes)
296:```
297:
298:## Example: How AI Creates OpenSpec Files
299:
300:When you ask your AI assistant to "add two-factor authentication", it creates:
301:
302:```
303:openspec/
304:├── specs/
305:│   └── auth/
306:│       └── spec.md           # Current auth spec (if exists)
307:└── changes/
308:    └── add-2fa/              # AI creates this entire structure
309:        ├── proposal.md       # Why and what changes
310:        ├── tasks.md          # Implementation checklist
311:        ├── design.md         # Technical decisions (optional)
312:        └── specs/
313:            └── auth/
314:                └── spec.md   # Delta showing additions
315:```
316:
317:### AI-Generated Spec (created in `openspec/specs/auth/spec.md`):
318:
319:```markdown
320:# Auth Specification
321:
322:## Purpose
323:Authentication and session management.
324:
325:## Requirements
326:### Requirement: User Authentication
327:The system SHALL issue a JWT on successful login.
328:
329:#### Scenario: Valid credentials
330:- WHEN a user submits valid credentials
331:- THEN a JWT is returned
332:```
333:
334:### AI-Generated Change Delta (created in `openspec/changes/add-2fa/specs/auth/spec.md`):
335:
336:```markdown
337:# Delta for Auth
338:
339:## ADDED Requirements
340:### Requirement: Two-Factor Authentication
341:The system MUST require a second factor during login.
342:
343:#### Scenario: OTP required
344:- WHEN a user submits valid credentials
345:- THEN an OTP challenge is required
346:```
347:
348:### AI-Generated Tasks (created in `openspec/changes/add-2fa/tasks.md`):
349:
350:```markdown
351:## 1. Database Setup
352:- [ ] 1.1 Add OTP secret column to users table
353:- [ ] 1.2 Create OTP verification logs table
354:
355:## 2. Backend Implementation  
356:- [ ] 2.1 Add OTP generation endpoint
357:- [ ] 2.2 Modify login flow to require OTP
358:- [ ] 2.3 Add OTP verification endpoint
359:
360:## 3. Frontend Updates
361:- [ ] 3.1 Create OTP input component
362:- [ ] 3.2 Update login flow UI
363:```
364:
365:**Important:** You don't create these files manually. Your AI assistant generates them based on your requirements and the existing codebase.
366:
367:## Understanding OpenSpec Files
368:
369:### Delta Format
370:
371:Deltas are "patches" that show how specs change:
372:
373:- **`## ADDED Requirements`** - New capabilities
374:- **`## MODIFIED Requirements`** - Changed behavior (include complete updated text)
375:- **`## REMOVED Requirements`** - Deprecated features
376:
377:**Format requirements:**
378:- Use `### Requirement: <name>` for headers
379:- Every requirement needs at least one `#### Scenario:` block
380:- Use SHALL/MUST in requirement text
381:
382:## How OpenSpec Compares
383:
384:### vs. spec-kit
385:OpenSpec’s two-folder model (`openspec/specs/` for the current truth, `openspec/changes/` for proposed updates) keeps state and diffs separate. This scales when you modify existing features or touch multiple specs. spec-kit is strong for greenfield/0→1 but provides less structure for cross-spec updates and evolving features.
386:
387:### vs. Kiro.dev
388:OpenSpec groups every change for a feature in one folder (`openspec/changes/feature-name/`), making it easy to track related specs, tasks, and designs together. Kiro spreads updates across multiple spec folders, which can make feature tracking harder.
389:
390:### vs. No Specs
391:Without specs, AI coding assistants generate code from vague prompts, often missing requirements or adding unwanted features. OpenSpec brings predictability by agreeing on the desired behavior before any code is written.
392:
393:## Team Adoption
394:
395:1. **Initialize OpenSpec** – Run `openspec init` in your repo.
396:2. **Start with new features** – Ask your AI to capture upcoming work as change proposals.
397:3. **Grow incrementally** – Each change archives into living specs that document your system.
398:4. **Stay flexible** – Different teammates can use Claude Code, CodeBuddy, Cursor, or any AGENTS.md-compatible tool while sharing the same specs.
399:
400:Run `openspec update` whenever someone switches tools so your agents pick up the latest instructions and slash-command bindings.
401:
402:## Updating OpenSpec
403:
404:1. **Upgrade the package**
405:   ```bash
406:   npm install -g @fission-ai/openspec@latest
407:   ```
408:2. **Refresh agent instructions**
409:   - Run `openspec update` inside each project to regenerate AI guidance and ensure the latest slash commands are active.
410:
411:## Experimental Features
412:
413:<details>
414:<summary><strong>🧪 OPSX: Fluid, Iterative Workflow</strong> (Claude Code only)</summary>
415:
416:**Why this exists:**
417:- Standard workflow is locked down — you can't tweak instructions or customize
418:- When AI output is bad, you can't improve the prompts yourself
419:- Same workflow for everyone, no way to match how your team works
420:
421:**What's different:**
422:- **Hackable** — edit templates and schemas yourself, test immediately, no rebuild
423:- **Granular** — each artifact has its own instructions, test and tweak individually
424:- **Customizable** — define your own workflows, artifacts, and dependencies
425:- **Fluid** — no phase gates, update any artifact anytime
426:
427:```
428:You can always go back:
429:
430:  proposal ──→ specs ──→ design ──→ tasks ──→ implement
431:     ▲           ▲          ▲                    │
432:     └───────────┴──────────┴────────────────────┘
433:```
434:
435:| Command | What it does |
436:|---------|--------------|
437:| `/opsx:new` | Start a new change |
438:| `/opsx:continue` | Create the next artifact (based on what's ready) |
439:| `/opsx:ff` | Fast-forward (all planning artifacts at once) |
440:| `/opsx:apply` | Implement tasks, updating artifacts as needed |
441:| `/opsx:archive` | Archive when done |
442:
443:**Setup:** `openspec experimental`
444:
445:[Full documentation →](docs/opsx.md)
446:
447:</details>
448:
449:<details>
450:<summary><strong>Telemetry</strong> – OpenSpec collects anonymous usage stats (opt-out: <code>OPENSPEC_TELEMETRY=0</code>)</summary>
451:
452:We collect only command names and version to understand usage patterns. No arguments, paths, content, or PII. Automatically disabled in CI.
453:
454:**Opt-out:** `export OPENSPEC_TELEMETRY=0` or `export DO_NOT_TRACK=1`

1:# CLI Reference
2:
3:The OpenSpec CLI (`openspec`) provides terminal commands for project setup, validation, status inspection, and management. These commands complement the AI slash commands (like `/opsx:propose`) documented in [Commands](commands.md).
4:
5:## Summary
6:
7:| Category | Commands | Purpose |
8:|----------|----------|---------|
9:| **Setup** | `init`, `update` | Initialize and update OpenSpec in your project |
10:| **Workspaces (beta)** | `workspace setup`, `workspace list`, `workspace ls`, `workspace link`, `workspace relink`, `workspace doctor`, `workspace open` | Set up planning across linked repos or folders |
11:| **Browsing** | `list`, `view`, `show` | Explore changes and specs |
12:| **Validation** | `validate` | Check changes and specs for issues |
13:| **Lifecycle** | `archive` | Finalize completed changes |
14:| **Workflow** | `status`, `instructions`, `templates`, `schemas` | Artifact-driven workflow support |
15:| **Schemas** | `schema init`, `schema fork`, `schema validate`, `schema which` | Create and manage custom workflows |
16:| **Config** | `config` | View and modify settings |
17:| **Utility** | `feedback`, `completion` | Feedback and shell integration |
18:
19:---
20:
21:## Human vs Agent Commands
22:
23:Most CLI commands are designed for **human use** in a terminal. Some commands also support **agent/script use** via JSON output.
24:
25:### Human-Only Commands
26:
27:These commands are interactive and designed for terminal use:
28:
29:| Command | Purpose |
30:|---------|---------|
31:| `openspec init` | Initialize project (interactive prompts) |
32:| `openspec view` | Interactive dashboard |
33:| `openspec config edit` | Open config in editor |
34:| `openspec feedback` | Submit feedback via GitHub |
35:| `openspec completion install` | Install shell completions |
36:
37:### Agent-Compatible Commands
38:
39:These commands support `--json` output for programmatic use by AI agents and scripts:
40:
41:| Command | Human Use | Agent Use |
42:|---------|-----------|-----------|
43:| `openspec list` | Browse changes/specs | `--json` for structured data |
44:| `openspec show <item>` | Read content | `--json` for parsing |
45:| `openspec validate` | Check for issues | `--all --json` for bulk validation |
46:| `openspec status` | See artifact progress | `--json` for structured status |
47:| `openspec instructions` | Get next steps | `--json` for agent instructions |
48:| `openspec templates` | Find template paths | `--json` for path resolution |
49:| `openspec schemas` | List available schemas | `--json` for schema discovery |
50:| `openspec workspace setup --no-interactive` | Create a workspace with explicit inputs | `--json` for structured setup output |
51:| `openspec workspace list` | Browse known workspaces | `--json` for typed workspace objects |
52:| `openspec workspace link` | Link a repo or folder | `--json` for structured link output |
53:| `openspec workspace relink` | Repair a linked path | `--json` for structured link output |
54:| `openspec workspace doctor` | Check one workspace | `--json` for structured status output |
55:

57:
58:## Global Options
59:
60:These options work with all commands:
61:
62:| Option | Description |
63:|--------|-------------|
64:| `--version`, `-V` | Show version number |
65:| `--no-color` | Disable color output |
66:| `--help`, `-h` | Display help for command |

68:---
69:
70:## Setup Commands
71:
72:### `openspec init`
73:
74:Initialize OpenSpec in your project. Creates the folder structure and configures AI tool integrations.
75:
76:Default behavior uses global config defaults: profile `core`, delivery `both`, workflows `propose, explore, apply, sync, archive`.
77:
78:```
79:openspec init [path] [options]
80:```
81:
82:**Arguments:**
83:
84:| Argument | Required | Description |
85:|----------|----------|-------------|
86:| `path` | No | Target directory (default: current directory) |
87:
88:**Options:**
89:
90:| Option | Description |
91:|--------|-------------|
92:| `--tools <list>` | Configure AI tools non-interactively. Use `all`, `none`, or comma-separated list |
93:| `--force` | Auto-cleanup legacy files without prompting |
94:| `--profile <profile>` | Override global profile for this init run (`core` or `custom`) |
95:
96:`--profile custom` uses whatever workflows are currently selected in global config (`openspec config profile`).
97:
98:**Supported tool IDs (`--tools`):** `amazon-q`, `antigravity`, `auggie`, `bob`, `claude`, `cline`, `codex`, `forgecode`, `codebuddy`, `continue`, `costrict`, `crush`, `cursor`, `factory`, `gemini`, `github-copilot`, `iflow`, `junie`, `kilocode`, `kimi`, `kiro`, `opencode`, `pi`, `qoder`, `lingma`, `qwen`, `roocode`, `trae`, `windsurf`
99:
100:**Examples:**
101:
102:```bash
103:# Interactive initialization
104:openspec init
105:
106:# Initialize in a specific directory
107:openspec init ./my-project
108:
109:# Non-interactive: configure for Claude and Cursor
110:openspec init --tools claude,cursor
111:
112:# Configure for all supported tools
113:openspec init --tools all
114:
115:# Override profile for this run
116:openspec init --profile core
117:
118:# Skip prompts and auto-cleanup legacy files
119:openspec init --force
120:```
121:
122:**What it creates:**
123:
124:```
125:openspec/
126:├── specs/              # Your specifications (source of truth)
127:├── changes/            # Proposed changes
128:└── config.yaml         # Project configuration
129:
130:.claude/skills/         # Claude Code skills (if claude selected)
131:.cursor/skills/         # Cursor skills (if cursor selected)
132:.cursor/commands/       # Cursor OPSX commands (if delivery includes commands)
133:... (other tool configs)
134:```
135:
136:---
137:
138:### `openspec update`
139:
140:Update OpenSpec instruction files after upgrading the CLI. Re-generates AI tool configuration files using your current global profile, selected workflows, and delivery mode.
141:
142:```
143:openspec update [path] [options]
144:```
145:
146:**Arguments:**
147:
148:| Argument | Required | Description |
149:|----------|----------|-------------|
150:| `path` | No | Target directory (default: current directory) |
151:
152:**Options:**
153:
154:| Option | Description |
155:|--------|-------------|
156:| `--force` | Force update even when files are up to date |
157:
158:**Example:**
159:
160:```bash
161:# Update instruction files after npm upgrade
162:npm update @fission-ai/openspec
163:openspec update
164:```
165:

167:
168:## Workspace Commands
169:
170:Workspace commands are under active development and are not ready for use yet. Do not build external automation, integrations, or long-lived workflows on top of this command surface; command behavior, state files, and JSON output can change at any point.
171:
172:Coordination workspaces are planning homes for work that spans multiple repos or folders. Workspace visibility is not change commitment: link the repos or folders OpenSpec should know about, then create changes when you are ready to plan specific work.
173:
174:### `openspec workspace setup`
175:
176:Create a workspace in the standard OpenSpec workspace location and link at least one existing repo or folder.
177:
178:```bash
179:openspec workspace setup [options]
180:```
181:
182:**Options:**
183:
184:| Option | Description |
185:|--------|-------------|
186:| `--name <name>` | Workspace name. Names must be kebab-case |
187:| `--link <path>` | Link an existing repo or folder and infer the link name from the folder name |
188:| `--link <name>=<path>` | Link an existing repo or folder with an explicit link name |
189:| `--opener <id>` | Store a preferred opener during non-interactive setup: `codex`, `claude`, `github-copilot`, or `editor` |
190:| `--no-interactive` | Disable prompts; requires `--name` and at least one `--link` |
191:| `--json` | Output JSON; requires `--no-interactive` |
192:
193:**Examples:**
194:
195:```bash
196:openspec workspace setup
197:openspec workspace setup --no-interactive --name platform --link /repos/api --link web=/repos/web
198:openspec workspace setup --no-interactive --name platform --link /repos/api --opener codex
199:openspec workspace setup --no-interactive --json --name checkout --link /repos/platform/apps/checkout
200:```
201:
202:Interactive setup asks for a preferred opener and stores it in machine-local workspace state. Non-interactive setup stores a preferred opener only when `--opener` is provided; otherwise `workspace open` prompts later in interactive terminals when a supported opener is available, or asks scripts to pass `--agent <tool>` or `--editor`.
203:
204:### `openspec workspace list`
205:
206:List known OpenSpec workspaces from the local registry.
207:
208:```bash
209:openspec workspace list [--json]
210:openspec workspace ls [--json]
211:```
212:
213:The list shows each workspace location and linked repos or folders. Stale registry records are reported but not changed.
214:
215:### `openspec workspace link`
216:
217:Record an existing repo or folder for one workspace.
218:
219:```bash
220:openspec workspace link [name] <path> [options]
221:```
222:
223:**Options:**
224:
225:| Option | Description |
226:|--------|-------------|
227:| `--workspace <name>` | Select a known workspace from the local registry |
228:| `--json` | Output JSON |
229:| `--no-interactive` | Disable workspace picker prompts |
230:
231:**Examples:**
232:
233:```bash
234:openspec workspace link /repos/api
235:openspec workspace link api-service /repos/api
236:openspec workspace link --workspace platform /repos/platform/apps/checkout
237:```
238:
239:The path must already exist. Relative paths are resolved against the command's current directory before OpenSpec stores the verified absolute path in machine-local workspace state. Linked paths can be full repos, packages, services, apps, or folders without repo-local `openspec/` state.
240:
241:### `openspec workspace relink`
242:
243:Repair or change the local path for an existing link.
244:
245:```bash
246:openspec workspace relink <name> <path> [options]
247:```
248:
249:The path must already exist. Relink updates only the machine-local path for the stable link name.
250:
251:### `openspec workspace doctor`
252:
253:Check what one workspace can resolve on the current machine.
254:
255:```bash
256:openspec workspace doctor [options]
257:```
258:
259:Doctor shows the workspace location, planning path, linked repos or folders, missing paths, repo-local specs paths when present, and suggested fixes. It reports issues only; it does not repair them automatically.
260:
261:Commands that need one workspace use the current workspace when run from inside a workspace folder or subdirectory. From elsewhere, pass `--workspace <name>`, select from the picker in an interactive terminal, or rely on the only known workspace when exactly one exists. In `--json` or `--no-interactive` mode, ambiguous selection fails with a structured status error and suggests `--workspace <name>`.
262:
263:JSON responses use typed objects plus `status` arrays. Primary data lives in `workspace`, `workspaces`, or `link`; warnings and errors live in `status`.
264:
265:### `openspec workspace open`
266:
267:Open a workspace working set through the stored preferred opener, a one-session agent override, or VS Code editor mode.
268:
269:```bash
270:openspec workspace open [name] [options]
271:```
272:
273:**Options:**
274:
275:| Option | Description |
276:|--------|-------------|
277:| `--workspace <name>` | Alias for the positional workspace name |
278:| `--agent <tool>` | One-session agent override: `codex`, `claude`, or `github-copilot` |
279:| `--editor` | Open the maintained VS Code workspace file as a normal editor workspace |
280:| `--no-interactive` | Disable workspace and opener picker prompts |
281:
282:**Examples:**
283:
284:```bash
285:openspec workspace open
286:openspec workspace open platform
287:openspec workspace open platform --agent github-copilot
288:openspec workspace open --agent codex
289:openspec workspace open --editor
290:```
291:
292:`workspace open` uses the current workspace when run inside one, auto-selects the only known workspace when run elsewhere, and asks the user to choose when multiple workspaces are known. `--agent` and `--editor` do not change the stored preferred opener. Passing both opener overrides is an error; choose either `--agent <tool>` or `--editor`.
293:
294:OpenSpec maintains `<workspace-name>.code-workspace` at the workspace root for VS Code editor and GitHub Copilot-in-VS-Code opens. That file is machine-local and ignored by default with a specific `<workspace-name>.code-workspace` `.gitignore` entry, so user-authored `*.code-workspace` files remain eligible for tracking.
295:
296:The maintained VS Code workspace includes the coordination root as `.` plus valid linked repos or folders as additional roots. VS Code displays those entries as a multi-root workspace.
297:
298:Root workspace open supports exploration and planning across linked repos or folders. Implementation edits should start only after an explicit user request and a normal OpenSpec implementation workflow.
299:

410:## Validation Commands
411:
412:### `openspec validate`
413:
414:Validate changes and specs for structural issues.
415:
416:```
417:openspec validate [item-name] [options]
418:```
419:
420:**Arguments:**
421:
422:| Argument | Required | Description |
423:|----------|----------|-------------|
424:| `item-name` | No | Specific item to validate (prompts if omitted) |
425:
426:**Options:**
427:
428:| Option | Description |
429:|--------|-------------|
430:| `--all` | Validate all changes and specs |
431:| `--changes` | Validate all changes |
432:| `--specs` | Validate all specs |
433:| `--type <type>` | Specify type when name is ambiguous: `change` or `spec` |
434:| `--strict` | Enable strict validation mode |
435:| `--json` | Output as JSON |
436:| `--concurrency <n>` | Max parallel validations (default: 6, or `OPENSPEC_CONCURRENCY` env) |
437:| `--no-interactive` | Disable prompts |
438:
439:**Examples:**
440:
441:```bash
442:# Interactive validation
443:openspec validate
444:
445:# Validate a specific change
446:openspec validate add-dark-mode
447:
448:# Validate all changes
449:openspec validate --changes
450:
451:# Validate everything with JSON output (for CI/scripts)
452:openspec validate --all --json
453:
454:# Strict validation with increased parallelism
455:openspec validate --all --strict --concurrency 12
456:```
457:
458:**Output (text):**
459:
460:```

462:  ✓ proposal.md valid
463:  ✓ specs/ui/spec.md valid
464:  ⚠ design.md: missing "Technical Approach" section
465:
466:1 warning found
467:```
468:
469:**Output (JSON):**
470:
471:```json

474:  "results": {
475:    "changes": [
476:      {
477:        "name": "add-dark-mode",
478:        "valid": true,
479:        "warnings": ["design.md: missing 'Technical Approach' section"]
480:      }
481:    ]
482:  },

485:    "valid": 1,
486:    "invalid": 0
487:  }
488:}

493:## Lifecycle Commands
494:
495:### `openspec archive`
496:
497:Archive a completed change and merge delta specs into main specs.
498:
499:```
500:openspec archive [change-name] [options]
501:```
502:
503:**Arguments:**
504:
505:| Argument | Required | Description |
506:|----------|----------|-------------|
507:| `change-name` | No | Change to archive (prompts if omitted) |
508:
509:**Options:**
510:
511:| Option | Description |
512:|--------|-------------|
513:| `-y, --yes` | Skip confirmation prompts |
514:| `--skip-specs` | Skip spec updates (for infrastructure/tooling/doc-only changes) |
515:| `--no-validate` | Skip validation (requires confirmation) |
516:
517:**Examples:**
518:
519:```bash
520:# Interactive archive
521:openspec archive
522:
523:# Archive specific change
524:openspec archive add-dark-mode
525:
526:# Archive without prompts (CI/scripts)
527:openspec archive add-dark-mode --yes
528:
529:# Archive a tooling change that doesn't affect specs
530:openspec archive update-ci-config --skip-specs
531:```
532:
533:**What it does:**
534:
535:1. Validates the change (unless `--no-validate`)
536:2. Prompts for confirmation (unless `--yes`)
537:3. Merges delta specs into `openspec/specs/`
538:4. Moves change folder to `openspec/changes/archive/YYYY-MM-DD-<name>/`
539:
